1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Mandatory bootstrap for UWP → WinUI 3 migration. Owns every mechanical step
4. LLMs reliably skip or get wrong. Run BEFORE any manual edit.
5. 
6. .DESCRIPTION
7. Each step below maps to the correspondingly-numbered `# ─── N.` section in
8. the code. Run `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` at the end of the SKILL.md
9. workflow to confirm residue grep, mapping integrity, deferred consistency,
10. build cleanliness, and runtime smoke.
11. 
12. Steps:
13. 1. Copy .xaml/.cs/.resw/asset/.appxmanifest from source to target, preserving folder structure
14. 2. Preserve the UWP .csproj at .uwp-source/ as a read-only reference
15. 3. Namespace mass-rewrite: Windows.UI.Xaml → Microsoft.UI.Xaml across all copied .cs/.xaml
16. 4a. Filter-prone class neutralization (RootFrameNavigationHelper → no-op stub, etc.)
17. 4b/4c. Per-file triage against unsupported-api-inventory.json + inline TODO injection
18.        (`// TODO[migrate-NNN]: see PATTERNS.md#<anchor>` — anchor-only, never an API name)
19. 5. Pre-seed MIGRATION-MAPPING.md (File / Triage / Status only — no Notes column to avoid
20.    loading the agent's context with concentrated API-name listings)
21. 6. Pre-seed MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md (generic anchor-based rationale, no API names)
22. 7. Write .bootstrap-meta.json (schema v2; includes perFileMode for BATCH vs SEQUENTIAL)
23. 8. Print a `=== BOOTSTRAP COMPLETE ===` summary block
24. 
25. Files with a sensitive-tier namespace (capture/speech/sensors/geolocation/
26. bluetooth/point-of-service/proximity) are marked SEQUENTIAL in perFileMode
27. (fix one TODO at a time, build after each). All other files are BATCH.
28. 
29. .PARAMETER Source
30. UWP project's C# source folder (contains the .csproj and Package.appxmanifest).
31. 
32. .PARAMETER Target
33. Scaffolded WinUI 3 target project root (e.g. produced by `dotnet new winui`).
34. 
35. .EXAMPLE
36. .\Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1 -Source "C:\src\UwpSample\cs" -Target "C:\out\MyWinUI3App"
37. #>
38. [CmdletBinding()]
39. param(
40.     [Parameter(Mandatory)][string]$Source,
41.     [Parameter(Mandatory)][string]$Target
42. )
43. 
44. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
45. 
46. function Resolve-FullPath([string]$p) {
47.     return (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $p).ProviderPath
48. }
49. 
50. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Source)) { throw "Source not found: $Source" }
51. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Target)) { throw "Target not found: $Target (scaffold the WinUI 3 project first with 'dotnet new winui')" }
52. 
53. $Source = Resolve-FullPath $Source
54. $Target = Resolve-FullPath $Target
55. 
56. Write-Host "==> Initialize-UwpMigration"
57. Write-Host "    Source : $Source"
58. Write-Host "    Target : $Target"
59. 
60. # ─── 1. Copy source files (everything except .csproj) ──────────────────────────
61. $patterns = @(
62.     '.xaml', '.cs', '.resw', '.resjson',
63.     '.appxmanifest',
64.     '.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.svg', '.ico', '.gif'
65. )
66. 
67. # Never copy build output / tooling folders from the UWP source. A previously-built
68. # UWP project leaves machine-generated sources under bin/ and obj/ (e.g. .NET-Native
69. # ILC *.g.cs in obj\<arch>\Release\ilc\). Those are NOT source: copying them in makes
70. # the SDK-style WinUI csproj's default `**/*.cs` glob compile thousands of duplicate
71. # types, and (for nested sub-project bin/obj) MSBuild's default obj/bin exclusion only
72. # covers the project-root obj/bin — nested ones are still compiled. Result: a build that
73. # fails or hangs with tens of thousands of CS0101/CS0227/CS0234 errors. This mirrors the
74. # $excludeDirs filter used by every other step below.
75. $srcExcludeDirs = @('bin', 'obj', '.vs', '.git', '.github', '.copilot', 'packages', 'node_modules')
76. $srcExcludePattern = '\\(' + ($srcExcludeDirs -join '|') + ')\\'
77. 
78. $copied = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.List[string]
79. 
80. Get-ChildItem -Path $Source -Recurse -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object {
81.     $rel = [System.IO.Path]::GetRelativePath($Source, $_.FullName)
82.     if (('\' + $rel) -match $srcExcludePattern) { return $false }
83.     $name = $_.Name.ToLowerInvariant()
84.     $match = $false
85.     foreach ($ext in $patterns) {
86.         if ($name.EndsWith($ext)) { $match = $true; break }
87.     }
88.     $match
89. } | ForEach-Object {
90.     $rel = [System.IO.Path]::GetRelativePath($Source, $_.FullName)
91.     $dst = Join-Path $Target $rel
92.     $dstDir = [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName($dst)
93.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $dstDir)) {
94.         New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $dstDir -Force | Out-Null
95.     }
96.     Copy-Item -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Destination $dst -Force
97.     [void]$copied.Add($rel)
98. }
99. 
100. Write-Host "    Copied $($copied.Count) source files"
101. 
102. # ─── 2. Preserve UWP .csproj as read-only reference ────────────────────────────
103. $uwpCsprojs = Get-ChildItem -Path $Source -Filter '*.csproj' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
104. $refDir = Join-Path $Target '.uwp-source'
105. if ($uwpCsprojs.Count -gt 0) {
106.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $refDir)) {
107.         New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $refDir -Force | Out-Null
108.     }
109.     foreach ($p in $uwpCsprojs) {
110.         $dst = Join-Path $refDir $p.Name
111.         Copy-Item -LiteralPath $p.FullName -Destination $dst -Force
112.         Write-Host "    Preserved $($p.Name) at .uwp-source/ (reference only — do not edit, do not include in build)"
113.     }
114. } else {
115.     Write-Warning "    No .csproj found under Source — agent has no reference for original PackageReference list"
116. }
117. 
118. # ─── 3. Namespace mass-replace: Windows.UI.Xaml → Microsoft.UI.Xaml ────────────
119. $excludeDirs = @('bin', 'obj', '.uwp-source', '.vs', '.git', '.github', '.copilot')
120. $excludePattern = '\\(' + ($excludeDirs -join '|') + ')\\'
121. $nsFiles = Get-ChildItem -Path $Target -Recurse -File -Include *.cs,*.xaml -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
122.     Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch $excludePattern }
123. $nsChanged = 0
124. foreach ($f in $nsFiles) {
125.     $orig = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($f.FullName)
126.     $new = $orig -replace 'Windows\.UI\.Xaml', 'Microsoft.UI.Xaml'
127.     if ($new -ne $orig) {
128.         [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($f.FullName, $new)
129.         $nsChanged++
130.     }
131. }
132. Write-Host "    Rewrote Windows.UI.Xaml -> Microsoft.UI.Xaml in $nsChanged of $($nsFiles.Count) .cs/.xaml files"
133. 
134. # ─── 4a. Filter-prone class neutralization ────────────────────────────────────
135. # Some SDK Samples boilerplate helpers contain UWP-specific patterns whose
136. # WinUI 3 equivalents require low-level Win32 keyboard interop. The model
137. # provider's content-safety filter routinely blocks model output containing
138. # those patterns and kills the trial. Replace those classes with no-op stubs
139. # *before* the agent ever sees them.
140. $filterProneClassNeutralizations = @(
141.     @{
142.         FilePattern  = '(^|\\)Common\\NavigationHelper\.cs$'
143.         ClassName    = 'RootFrameNavigationHelper'
144.         Reason       = 'Filter-prone: keyboard back-nav handler. WinUI 3 equivalent requires Microsoft.UI.Input keyboard-state APIs that trigger the content-safety filter. Replaced with no-op stub.'
145.         StubBody     = @'
146.         // No-op stub written by Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1.
147.         //
148.         // The original UWP implementation hooked accelerator-key activation for
149.         // ALT+Left/Right and BrowserBack/Forward, and pointer events for mouse
150.         // XButton1/XButton2. The WinUI 3 equivalent goes through low-level
151.         // keyboard-state APIs that the model provider's content-safety filter
152.         // rejects. Back-nav is not the demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK
153.         // sample, so we leave the field bound but make activation a no-op.
154.         // If you really need ALT+Left back-nav, add a single
155.         //   <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
156.         // to the AppBarButton or NavigationViewItem that triggers GoBack.
157. '@
158.     }
159. )
160. 
161. function Invoke-FilterProneNeutralization {
162.     param(
163.         [string]$FullPath,
164.         [string]$ClassName,
165.         [string]$StubBody,
166.         [string]$Reason
167.     )
168.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $FullPath)) { return $false }
169.     $text = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($FullPath)
170.     $pattern = "(?ms)((?:public\s+|internal\s+)?class\s+$([regex]::Escape($ClassName))\b[^{]*\{)"
171.     $m = [regex]::Match($text, $pattern)
172.     if (-not $m.Success) { return $false }
173.     $bodyStart = $m.Index + $m.Length
174.     $depth = 1
175.     $i = $bodyStart
176.     while ($i -lt $text.Length -and $depth -gt 0) {
177.         $ch = $text[$i]
178.         if ($ch -eq '{') { $depth++ }
179.         elseif ($ch -eq '}') { $depth-- }
180.         $i++
181.     }
182.     if ($depth -ne 0) { return $false }
183.     $bodyEnd = $i - 1
184.     $before = $text.Substring(0, $bodyStart)
185.     $after = $text.Substring($bodyEnd)
186.     $stubCtor = "        public $ClassName(params object[] args) { /* no-op; accepts any call shape */ }`r`n"
187.     $newText = $before + "`r`n" + $StubBody + "`r`n" + $stubCtor + "    " + $after
188.     [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($FullPath, $newText)
189.     return $true
190. }
191. 
192. $neutralizedFiles = @{}
193. foreach ($n in $filterProneClassNeutralizations) {
194.     foreach ($rel in $copied) {
195.         if ($rel -notmatch $n.FilePattern) { continue }
196.         $full = Join-Path $Target $rel
197.         $ok = Invoke-FilterProneNeutralization -FullPath $full -ClassName $n.ClassName -StubBody $n.StubBody -Reason $n.Reason
198.         if ($ok) {
199.             $key = $rel
200.             if (-not $neutralizedFiles.ContainsKey($key)) { $neutralizedFiles[$key] = @() }
201.             $neutralizedFiles[$key] += $n.ClassName
202.             Write-Host "    Neutralized class $($n.ClassName) in $rel"
203.         }
204.     }
205. }
206. 
207. # ─── 4b. Load inventory ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
208. $invPath = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'unsupported-api-inventory.json'
209. $inv = $null
210. if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $invPath) {
211.     try { $inv = Get-Content -LiteralPath $invPath -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json } catch { Write-Warning "Failed to parse $invPath - skipping pre-triage" }
212. }
213. 
214. # Adaptable patterns with anchor (for TODO injection). Each entry: { name, pattern, anchor, tier }
215. $adaptableEntries = @()
216. if ($inv -and $inv.adaptable) {
217.     foreach ($e in $inv.adaptable) {
218.         if (-not $e.anchor) { continue }
219.         $adaptableEntries += [PSCustomObject]@{
220.             Name    = $e.name
--- RESULT ---

1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Fetch a single anchored section from MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md.
4. 
5. .DESCRIPTION
6. Use this instead of opening MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md wholesale — the full file's
7. concentrated API-name listings have historically tripped the model provider's
8. content-safety filter. This helper returns only the requested section.
9. 
10. .PARAMETER Anchor
11. Anchor ID (e.g. 'threading', 'windowing', 'dialogs', 'pickers'). The full
12. list lives in unsupported-api-inventory.json under each entry's `anchor`.
13. 
14. .PARAMETER PatternsPath
15. Optional path to MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md. Defaults to the sibling file under
16. the winui-uwp-migration skill folder.
17. 
18. .OUTPUTS
19. The section text (heading + content up to the next ## heading) on stdout.
20. Exits 0 on success, 1 if the anchor is not found.
21. 
22. .EXAMPLE
23. .\Get-MigrationPattern.ps1 -Anchor threading
24. #>
25. [CmdletBinding()]
26. param(
27.     [Parameter(Mandatory)][string]$Anchor,
28.     [string]$PatternsPath
29. )
30. 
31. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
32. 
33. if (-not $PatternsPath) {
34.     $PatternsPath = Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot) 'MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md'
35. }
36. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $PatternsPath)) {
37.     Write-Error "MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md not found at $PatternsPath"
38.     exit 1
39. }
40. 
41. $lines = Get-Content -LiteralPath $PatternsPath
42. $anchorPattern = "<a\s+id=`"$([regex]::Escape($Anchor))`""
43. 
44. $startIdx = -1
45. for ($i = 0; $i -lt $lines.Count; $i++) {
46.     if ($lines[$i] -match $anchorPattern) {
47.         $startIdx = $i
48.         break
49.     }
50. }
51. 
52. if ($startIdx -lt 0) {
53.     Write-Error "Anchor '#$Anchor' not found in $PatternsPath"
54.     exit 1
55. }
56. 
57. # Walk forward: skip the anchor line itself and the heading; collect until the next top-level ##.
58. $endIdx = $lines.Count - 1
59. for ($j = $startIdx + 1; $j -lt $lines.Count; $j++) {
60.     # Stop on next anchor (covers cases where two anchors precede consecutive sections)
61.     if ($lines[$j] -match '<a\s+id="' -and $j -gt $startIdx) {
62.         $endIdx = $j - 1
63.         break
64.     }
65.     # Stop on next top-level heading
66.     if ($lines[$j] -match '^##\s' -and $j -gt $startIdx + 1) {
67.         $endIdx = $j - 1
68.         break
69.     }
70. }
71. 
72. # Trim trailing blank lines for cleaner output
73. while ($endIdx -gt $startIdx -and [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($lines[$endIdx])) {
74.     $endIdx--
75. }
76. 
77. for ($k = $startIdx; $k -le $endIdx; $k++) {
78.     Write-Output $lines[$k]
79. }
80.

156.         /// <summary>
157.         /// This is event handler for 'Extract' button.
158.         /// Captures image from camera ,recognizes text and displays it.
159.         /// </summary>
160.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
161.         /// <param name="e"></param>
162.         private async void ExtractButton_Click(object sender, Microsoft.UI.Xaml.RoutedEventArgs e)
163.         {
164.             //Get information about the preview.
165.             var previewProperties = mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.GetMediaStreamProperties(MediaStreamType.VideoPreview) as VideoEncodingProperties;
166.             int videoFrameWidth = (int)previewProperties.Width;
167.             int videoFrameHeight = (int)previewProperties.Height;
168. 
169.             // In portrait modes, the width and height must be swapped for the VideoFrame to have the correct aspect ratio and avoid letterboxing / black bars.
170.             if (!externalCamera && (displayInformation.CurrentOrientation == DisplayOrientations.Portrait || displayInformation.CurrentOrientation == DisplayOrientations.PortraitFlipped))
171.             {
172.                 videoFrameWidth = (int)previewProperties.Height;
173.                 videoFrameHeight = (int)previewProperties.Width;
174.             }
175. 
176.             // Create the video frame to request a SoftwareBitmap preview frame.
177.             var videoFrame = new VideoFrame(BitmapPixelFormat.Bgra8, videoFrameWidth, videoFrameHeight);
178. 
179.             // Capture the preview frame.
180.             using (var currentFrame = await mediaCapture.GetPreviewFrameAsync(videoFrame))
181.             {
182.                 // Collect the resulting frame.
183.                 SoftwareBitmap bitmap = currentFrame.SoftwareBitmap;
184. 
185.                 OcrEngine ocrEngine = OcrEngine.TryCreateFromLanguage(ocrLanguage);
186. 
187.                 if (ocrEngine == null)
188.                 {
189.                     rootPage.NotifyUser(ocrLanguage.DisplayName + " is not supported.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
190. 
191.                     return;
192.                 }
193. 
194.                 var imgSource = new WriteableBitmap(bitmap.PixelWidth, bitmap.PixelHeight);
195.                 bitmap.CopyToBuffer(imgSource.PixelBuffer);
196.                 PreviewImage.Source = imgSource;
197. 
198.                 var ocrResult = await ocrEngine.RecognizeAsync(bitmap);
199. 
200.                 // Used for text overlay.
201.                 // Prepare scale transform for words since image is not displayed in original format.
202.                 var scaleTrasform = new ScaleTransform
203.                 {
204.                     CenterX = 0,
205.                     CenterY = 0,
206.                     ScaleX = PreviewControl.ActualWidth / bitmap.PixelWidth,
207.                     ScaleY = PreviewControl.ActualHeight / bitmap.PixelHeight
208.                 };
209. 
210.                 if (ocrResult.TextAngle != null)
211.                 {
212.                     // If text is detected under some angle in this sample scenario we want to
213.                     // overlay word boxes over original image, so we rotate overlay boxes.
214.                     TextOverlay.RenderTransform = new RotateTransform
215.                     {
216.                         Angle = (double)ocrResult.TextAngle,
217.                         CenterX = PreviewImage.ActualWidth / 2,
218.                         CenterY = PreviewImage.ActualHeight / 2
219.                     };
220.                 }

30.     public sealed partial class OcrFileImage : Page
31.     {
32.         // A pointer back to the main page.
33.         // This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such as NotifyUser()
34.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
35. 
36.         // Bitmap holder of currently loaded image.
37.         private SoftwareBitmap bitmap;
38. 
39.         // Recognized words overlay boxes.
40.         private List<WordOverlay> wordBoxes = new List<WordOverlay>();
41. 
42.         public OcrFileImage()
43.         {
44.             this.InitializeComponent();
45.         }
46. 
47.         /// <summary>
48.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
49.         /// Updates OCR available languages and loads sample image.
50.         /// </summary>
51.         /// <param name="e"></param>
52.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
53.         {
54.             UpdateAvailableLanguages();
55. 
56.             await LoadSampleImageAsync();
57.         }
58. 
59.         /// <summary>
60.         /// Updates list of ocr languages available on device.
61.         /// </summary>
62.         private void UpdateAvailableLanguages()
63.         {
64.             if (!UserLanguageToggle.IsOn)
65.             {
66.                 // Check if any OCR language is available on device.
67.                 if (OcrEngine.AvailableRecognizerLanguages.Count > 0)
68.                 {
69.                     LanguageList.DisplayMemberPath = "DisplayName";
70.                     LanguageList.ItemsSource = OcrEngine.AvailableRecognizerLanguages;
71.                     LanguageList.SelectedIndex = 0;
72.                     LanguageList.IsEnabled = true;
73.                 }
74.                 else
75.                 {
76.                     // Prevent OCR if no OCR languages are available on device.
77.                     UserLanguageToggle.IsEnabled = false;
78.                     LanguageList.IsEnabled = false;
79.                     ExtractButton.IsEnabled = false;
80. 
81.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("No available OCR languages.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
82.                 }
83.             }
84.             else
85.             {
86.                 LanguageList.ItemsSource = null;
87.                 LanguageList.IsEnabled = false;
88. 
89.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(
90.                     "Run OCR in first OCR available language from UserProfile.GlobalizationPreferences.Languages list.",
91.                     NotifyType.StatusMessage);
92.             }
93.         }
94. 
95.         /// <summary>
96.         /// Loads image from file to bitmap and displays it in UI.
97.         /// </summary>
98.         /// <param name="file"></param>
99.         /// <returns></returns>
100.         private async Task LoadImageAsync(StorageFile file)
101.         {
102.             using (var stream = await file.OpenAsync(Windows.Storage.FileAccessMode.Read))
103.             {
104.                 var decoder = await BitmapDecoder.CreateAsync(stream);
105. 
106.                 bitmap = await decoder.GetSoftwareBitmapAsync(BitmapPixelFormat.Bgra8, BitmapAlphaMode.Premultiplied);
107. 
108.                 var imgSource = new WriteableBitmap(bitmap.PixelWidth, bitmap.PixelHeight);
109.                 bitmap.CopyToBuffer(imgSource.PixelBuffer);
110.                 PreviewImage.Source = imgSource;
111.             }
112.         }
113. 
114.         private async Task LoadSampleImageAsync()
115.         {
116.             var file = await Windows.ApplicationModel.Package.Current.InstalledLocation.GetFileAsync("Assets\\splash-sdk.png");
117.             await LoadImageAsync(file);
118.         }
119. 
120.         /// <summary>
121.         /// Clears extracted text and text overlay from previous OCR.
122.         /// </summary>
123.         private void ClearResults()
124.         {
125.             TextOverlay.RenderTransform = null;
126.             ExtractedTextBox.Text = String.Empty;
127.             TextOverlay.Children.Clear();
128.             wordBoxes.Clear();
129.         }
130. 
131.         /// <summary>
132.         ///  Update word box transform to match current UI size.
133.         /// </summary>
134.         private void UpdateWordBoxTransform()
135.         {
136.             // Used for text overlay.
137.             // Prepare scale transform for words since image is not displayed in original size.
138.             ScaleTransform scaleTrasform = new ScaleTransform
139.             {
140.                 CenterX = 0,
141.                 CenterY = 0,
142.                 ScaleX = PreviewImage.ActualWidth / bitmap.PixelWidth,
143.                 ScaleY = PreviewImage.ActualHeight / bitmap.PixelHeight
144.             };
145. 
146.             foreach (var item in wordBoxes)
147.             {
148.                 item.Transform(scaleTrasform);
149.             }
150.         }
151. 
152.         /// <summary>
153.         /// This is event handler for 'Extract' button.
154.         /// Recognizes text from image and displays it.
155.         /// </summary>
156.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
157.         /// <param name="e"></param>
158.         private async void ExtractButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
159.         {
160.             ClearResults();
161. 
162.             // Check if OcrEngine supports image resolution.
163.             if (bitmap.PixelWidth > OcrEngine.MaxImageDimension || bitmap.PixelHeight > OcrEngine.MaxImageDimension)
164.             {
165.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(
166.                     String.Format("Bitmap dimensions ({0}x{1}) are too big for OCR.", bitmap.PixelWidth, bitmap.PixelHeight) +
167.                     "Max image dimension is " + OcrEngine.MaxImageDimension + ".",
168.                     NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
169. 
170.                 return;
171.             }
172. 
173.             OcrEngine ocrEngine = null;
174. 
175.             if (UserLanguageToggle.IsOn)
176.             {
177.                 // Try to create OcrEngine for first supported language from UserProfile.GlobalizationPreferences.Languages list.
178.                 // If none of the languages are available on device, method returns null.
179.                 ocrEngine = OcrEngine.TryCreateFromUserProfileLanguages();
180.             }
181.             else
182.             {
183.                 // Try to create OcrEngine for specified language.
184.                 // If language is not supported on device, method returns null.
185.                 ocrEngine = OcrEngine.TryCreateFromLanguage(LanguageList.SelectedValue as Language);
186.             }
187. 
188.             if (ocrEngine != null)
189.             {
190.                 // Recognize text from image.
191.                 var ocrResult = await ocrEngine.RecognizeAsync(bitmap);
192. 
193.                 // Display recognized text.
194.                 ExtractedTextBox.Text = ocrResult.Text;
195. 
196.                 if (ocrResult.TextAngle != null)
197.                 {
198.                     // If text is detected under some angle in this sample scenario we want to
199.                     // overlay word boxes over original image, so we rotate overlay boxes.
200.                     TextOverlay.RenderTransform = new RotateTransform
201.                     {
202.                         Angle = (double)ocrResult.TextAngle,
203.                         CenterX = PreviewImage.ActualWidth / 2,
204.                         CenterY = PreviewImage.ActualHeight / 2
205.                     };
206.                 }
207. 
208.                 // Create overlay boxes over recognized words.
209.                 foreach (var line in ocrResult.Lines)
210.                 {
211.                     // Determine if line is horizontal or vertical.
212.                     // Vertical lines are supported only in Chinese Traditional and Japanese languages.
213.                     Rect lineRect = Rect.Empty;
214.                     foreach (var word in line.Words)
215.                     {
216.                         lineRect.Union(word.BoundingRect);
217.                     }
218.                     bool isVerticalLine = lineRect.Height > lineRect.Width;
219.                     var style = isVerticalLine ? HighlightedWordBoxVerticalLineStyle : HighlightedWordBoxHorizontalLineStyle;
220. 
221.                     foreach (var word in line.Words)
222.                     {
223.                         WordOverlay wordBoxOverlay = new WordOverlay(word);
224. 
225.                         // Keep references to word boxes.
226.                         wordBoxes.Add(wordBoxOverlay);
227. 
228.                         // Create a box to highlight the word.
229.                         TextOverlay.Children.Add(wordBoxOverlay.CreateBorder(style));
230.                     }
231.                 }
232. 
233.                 // Rescale word boxes to match current UI size.
234.                 UpdateWordBoxTransform();
235. 
236.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(
237.                     "Image is OCRed for " + ocrEngine.RecognizerLanguage.DisplayName + " language.",
238.                     NotifyType.StatusMessage);
239.             }
240.             else
241.             {
242.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Selected language is not available.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
243.             }
244.         }
245.

247.         /// Occurs when user language toggle state is changed.
248.         /// </summary>

250.         /// <param name="e"></param>
251.         private void UserLanguageToggle_Toggled(object sender, Microsoft.UI.Xaml.RoutedEventArgs e)
252.         {
253.             UpdateAvailableLanguages();
254.         }

271.         /// <summary>
272.         /// This is event handler for 'Load' button.
273.         /// It opens file picked and load selected image in UI..
274.         /// </summary>
275.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
276.         /// <param name="e"></param>
277.         private async void LoadButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
278.         {
279.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#pickers
280.             var picker = new FileOpenPicker()
281.             {
282.                 SuggestedStartLocation = PickerLocationId.PicturesLibrary,
283.                 FileTypeFilter = { ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".png" },
284.             };
285. 
286.             var file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
287.             if (file != null)
288.             {
289.                 ClearResults();
290. 
291.                 await LoadImageAsync(file);
292. 
293.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(
294.                     String.Format("Loaded image from file: {0} ({1}x{2}).", file.Name, bitmap.PixelWidth, bitmap.PixelHeight),
295.                     NotifyType.StatusMessage);
296.             }
297.         }

299.         /// <summary>
300.         /// Occurs when selected language is changed in available languages combo box.
301.         /// </summary>
302.         /// <param name="sender"></param>

304.         private void LanguageList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)

309.         /// <summary>
310.         /// Occurs when displayed image size changes.
311.         /// </summary>
312.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
313.         /// <param name="e"></param>
314.         private void PreviewImage_SizeChanged(object sender, SizeChangedEventArgs e)
315.         {
316.             // Update word overlay boxes.
317.             UpdateWordBoxTransform();
318. 
319.             // Update image rotation center.
320.             var rotate = TextOverlay.RenderTransform as RotateTransform;
321.             if (rotate != null)
322.             {
323.                 rotate.CenterX = PreviewImage.ActualWidth / 2;
324.                 rotate.CenterY = PreviewImage.ActualHeight / 2;
325.             }
326.         }
327.     }
328. }
329. 
--- RESULT ---
430. 
431.         /// <summary>
432.         /// Gets the current orientation of the UI in relation to the device and applies a corrective rotation to the preview.
433.         /// </summary>
434.         private async Task SetPreviewRotationAsync()
435.         {
436.             // Only need to update the orientation if the camera is mounted on the device.
437.             if (externalCamera) return;
438. 
439.             // Calculate which way and how far to rotate the preview.
440.             int rotationDegrees;
441.             VideoRotation sourceRotation;
442.             CalculatePreviewRotation(out sourceRotation, out rotationDegrees);
443. 
444.             // Set preview rotation in the preview source.
445.             mediaCapture.SetPreviewRotation(sourceRotation);
446. 
447.             // Add rotation metadata to the preview stream to make sure the aspect ratio / dimensions match when rendering and getting preview frames
448.             var props = mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.GetMediaStreamProperties(MediaStreamType.VideoPreview);
449.             props.Properties.Add(RotationKey, rotationDegrees);
450.             await mediaCapture.SetEncodingPropertiesAsync(MediaStreamType.VideoPreview, props, null);
451.         }
452. 
453.         /// <summary>
454.         /// Stops the preview and deactivates a display request, to allow the screen to go into power saving modes, and locks the UI
455.         /// </summary>
456.         /// <returns></returns>
457.         private async Task StopPreviewAsync()
458.         {
459.             try
460.             {
461.                 isPreviewing = false;
462.                 await mediaCapture.StopPreviewAsync();
463.             }
464.             catch (Exception ex)
465.             {
466.                 // Use the dispatcher because this method is sometimes called from non-UI threads.
467.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
468.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
469.                 {
470.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Exception stopping preview. " + ex.Message, NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
471.                 });
472.             }
473. 
474.             // Use the dispatcher because this method is sometimes called from non-UI threads.
475.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
476.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
477.             {
478.                 PreviewControl.Source = null;
479. 
480.                 // Allow the device to sleep now that the preview is stopped.
481.                 displayRequest.RequestRelease();
482.             });
483.         }
484. 
485.         /// <summary>
486.         /// Cleans up the camera resources (after stopping the preview if necessary) and unregisters from MediaCapture events.
487.         /// </summary>
488.         private async Task CleanupCameraAsync()
489.         {
490.             if (isInitialized)
491.             {
492.                 if (isPreviewing)
493.                 {
494.                     // The call to stop the preview is included here for completeness, but can be
495.                     // safely removed if a call to MediaCapture.Dispose() is being made later,
496.                     // as the preview will be automatically stopped at that point
497.                     await StopPreviewAsync();
498.                 }
499. 
500.                 isInitialized = false;
501.             }
502. 
503.             if (mediaCapture != null)
504.             {
505.                 mediaCapture.Failed -= MediaCapture_Failed;
506.                 mediaCapture.Dispose();
507.                 mediaCapture = null;
508.             }
509.         }
510. 
511.         /// <summary>
512.         /// Queries the available video capture devices to try and find one mounted on the desired panel.
513.         /// </summary>
514.         /// <param name="desiredPanel">The panel on the device that the desired camera is mounted on.</param>
515.         /// <returns>A DeviceInformation instance with a reference to the camera mounted on the desired panel if available,
516.         ///          any other camera if not, or null if no camera is available.</returns>
517.         private static async Task<DeviceInformation> FindCameraDeviceByPanelAsync(Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel desiredPanel)
518.         {
519.             // Get available devices for capturing pictures.
520.             var allVideoDevices = await DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(DeviceClass.VideoCapture);
521. 
522.             // Get the desired camera by panel.
523.             DeviceInformation desiredDevice = allVideoDevices.FirstOrDefault(x => x.EnclosureLocation != null && x.EnclosureLocation.Panel == desiredPanel);
524. 
525.             // If there is no device mounted on the desired panel, return the first device found.
526.             return desiredDevice ?? allVideoDevices.FirstOrDefault();
527.         }
528. 
529.         /// <summary>
530.         /// Reads the current orientation of the app and calculates the VideoRotation necessary to ensure the preview is rendered in the correct orientation.
531.         /// </summary>
532.         /// <param name="sourceRotation">The rotation value to use in MediaCapture.SetPreviewRotation.</param>
533.         /// <param name="rotationDegrees">The accompanying rotation metadata with which to tag the preview stream.</param>
534.         private void CalculatePreviewRotation(out VideoRotation sourceRotation, out int rotationDegrees)
535.         {
536.             // Note that in some cases, the rotation direction needs to be inverted if the preview is being mirrored.
537. 
538.             switch (displayInformation.CurrentOrientation)
539.             {
540.                 case DisplayOrientations.Portrait:
541.                     if (mirroringPreview)
542.                     {
543.                         rotationDegrees = 270;
544.                         sourceRotation = VideoRotation.Clockwise270Degrees;
545.                     }
546.                     else
547.                     {
548.                         rotationDegrees = 90;
549.                         sourceRotation = VideoRotation.Clockwise90Degrees;
550.                     }
551.                     break;
552. 
553.                 case DisplayOrientations.LandscapeFlipped:
554.                     // No need to invert this rotation, as rotating 180 degrees is the same either way.
555.                     rotationDegrees = 180;
556.                     sourceRotation = VideoRotation.Clockwise180Degrees;
557.                     break;
558. 
559.                 case DisplayOrientations.PortraitFlipped:
560.                     if (mirroringPreview)
561.                     {
562.                         rotationDegrees = 90;
563.                         sourceRotation = VideoRotation.Clockwise90Degrees;
564.                     }
565.                     else
566.                     {
567.                         rotationDegrees = 270;
568.                         sourceRotation = VideoRotation.Clockwise270Degrees;
569.                     }
570.                     break;
571. 
572.                 case DisplayOrientations.Landscape:
573.                 default:
574.                     rotationDegrees = 0;
575.                     sourceRotation = VideoRotation.None;
576.                     break;
577.             }
578.         }
579. 
580.         /// <summary>
581.         /// Handles MediaCapture failures. Cleans up the camera resources.
582.         /// </summary>
583.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
584.         /// <param name="errorEventArgs"></param>
585.         private async void MediaCapture_Failed(MediaCapture sender, MediaCaptureFailedEventArgs errorEventArgs)
586.         {
587.             await CleanupCameraAsync();
588. 
589.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
590.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
591.             {
592.                 ExtractButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
593.                 CameraButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
594. 
595.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("MediaCapture Failed. " + errorEventArgs.Message, NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
596.             });
597.         }
598. 
599.         #endregion MediaCapture methods
600.     }
601. }

<a id="getforcurrentview"></a>
## GetForCurrentView() Replacements

The `GetForCurrentView()` *entry point* is gone in WinUI 3 desktop — there is no implicit per-view singleton. But most of the underlying objects still exist; replace `GetForCurrentView()` with a **window-scoped factory** (or an `AppWindow` property), do **not** conclude the feature is unsupported and degrade to a `try/catch` stub.

| UWP API | WinUI 3 Replacement |
|---------|---------------------|
| `ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId)` |
| `UIViewSettings.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow` properties (size, presenter) |
| `DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` | `Microsoft.Graphics.Display.DisplayInformation.CreateForWindowId(windowId)` — keeps `NativeOrientation` / `CurrentOrientation` / DPI / color members. (For DPI *only*, `XamlRoot.RasterizationScale` or Win32 `GetDpiForWindow` is a shortcut.) |
| `CoreApplication.GetCurrentView()` | Track windows manually in `App` |
| `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView()` | Wire back handling in `NavigationView` / `BackRequested` directly |

`DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` **throws** on desktop (`GetForCurrentView must be called on a thread associated with a CoreWindow`) — swap it for the window-scoped factory rather than catching the exception. `CreateForWindowId` must run on a thread with a running `DispatcherQueue`; cache the instance and de-register its events.

```csharp
using Microsoft.Graphics.Display; // WinApp SDK DisplayInformation
using Microsoft.UI;               // Win32Interop

var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
var windowId = Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var displayInfo = DisplayInformation.CreateForWindowId(windowId);
var current = displayInfo.CurrentOrientation; // DisplayOrientations
var native  = displayInfo.NativeOrientation;
```

---ANCHOR---

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).

---ANCHOR---

<a id="pickers"></a>
## Pickers and Win32 Surfaces

UWP pickers infer the active window. WinUI 3 desktop pickers must be initialized explicitly or `ShowAsync` throws `COMException`.

```csharp
var picker = new FileOpenPicker();
picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".txt");
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
WinRT.Interop.InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(picker, hwnd);
var file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
```

Apply the same `InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(obj, hwnd)` pattern to `FolderPicker`, `FileSavePicker`, `DataTransferManager` (Share), `PrintManager`, and other UI surfaces that target a window.

---ANCHOR---

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates

- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.

### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:

- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.

Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.

### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually have

The WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).

If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:

```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```

(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)

You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:

- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.

- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.

Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires runFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capability>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matter what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern).

5. **Carry over every UWP `<Extension>` the app's behavior depends on.** The scaffold manifest has none; the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` are activation/registration prerequisites — omitting them makes the feature fail **at runtime** (often as a silently dead control), not at build time. Copy each `<Extension>` block from the UWP source manifest (under `.uwp-source/` or the copied UWP `Package.appxmanifest`) into the WinUI 3 root manifest's `<Application>`. Common ones:

   | Extension `Category` | Feature it powers |
   |----------------------|-------------------|
   | `windows.backgroundTasks` | out-of-process `BackgroundTaskBuilder` tasks (one `<Extension EntryPoint="...">` per `TaskEntryPoint`) |
   | `windows.protocol` | custom URI-scheme activation |
   | `windows.fileTypeAssociation` | open-with / file activation |
   | `windows.appService` | background app services |
   | `windows.shareTarget` | receiving shared content |

   `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` FAILs if retained `BackgroundTaskBuilder` code has no `windows.backgroundTasks` extension; the other categories have no automated check, so verify them by diffing the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` against the migrated one.

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Threading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Search for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires ALT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalent requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code constants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Migrating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites just the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHelper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavigationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, the LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// Wraps recognized word and provides word overlay position and size based on current scale.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     class WordOverlay : INotifyPropertyChanged
26.     {
27.         private OcrWord word;
28.         private Rect wordBoundingRect;
29. 
30.         /// <summary>
31.         /// Left and Right properties of Thickess define word box position.
32.         /// </summary>
33.         public Thickness WordPosition => new Thickness(wordBoundingRect.Left, wordBoundingRect.Top, 0, 0);
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Scaled word box width.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         public double WordWidth => wordBoundingRect.Width;
39. 
40.         /// <summary>
41.         /// Scaled word box height.
42.         /// </summary>
43.         public double WordHeight => wordBoundingRect.Height;
44. 
45.         public event PropertyChangedEventHandler PropertyChanged;
46. 
47.         public WordOverlay(OcrWord ocrWord)
48.         {
49.             word = ocrWord;
50. 
51.             UpdateProps(word.BoundingRect);
52.         }
53. 
54.         public void Transform(ScaleTransform scale)
55.         {
56.             // Scale word box bounding rect and update properties.
57.             UpdateProps(scale.TransformBounds(word.BoundingRect));
58.         }
59. 
60.         public Border CreateBorder(Style style, UIElement child = null)
61.         {
62.             var overlay = new Border()
63.             {
64.                 Child = child,
65.                 Style = style
66.             };
67. 
68.             // Bind word boxes to UI.
69.             overlay.SetBinding(FrameworkElement.MarginProperty, CreateBinding("WordPosition"));
70.             overlay.SetBinding(FrameworkElement.WidthProperty, CreateBinding("WordWidth"));
71.             overlay.SetBinding(FrameworkElement.HeightProperty, CreateBinding("WordHeight"));
72. 
73.             return overlay;
74.         }
75. 
76.         Binding CreateBinding(string propertyName) => new Binding() { Path = new PropertyPath(propertyName), Source = this };
77. 
78.         private void UpdateProps(Rect wordBoundingBox)
79.         {
80.             wordBoundingRect = wordBoundingBox;
81. 
82.             OnPropertyChanged("WordPosition");
83.             OnPropertyChanged("WordWidth");
84.             OnPropertyChanged("WordHeight");
85.         }
86. 
87.         protected void OnPropertyChanged(string PropertyName)
88.         {
89.             PropertyChanged?.Invoke(this, new PropertyChangedEventArgs(PropertyName));
90.         }
91.     }
92. }

12. using System;
13. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Activation;
14. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
15. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
16. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
17. 
18. // The Blank Application template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402347&clcid=0x409
19. 
20. namespace SDKTemplate
21. {
22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// Provides application-specific behavior to supplement the default Application class.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     sealed partial class App : Application
26.     {
27.         /// <summary>
28.         /// Initializes the singleton application object.  This is the first line of authored code
29.         /// executed, and as such is the logical equivalent of main() or WinMain().
30.         /// </summary>
31.         public App()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.             this.Construct();
35.         }
36. 
37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Invoked when the application is launched normally by the end user.  Other entry points
39.         /// will be used such as when the application is launched to open a specific file.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>
42.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
99. 
100.                 // Set the default language
101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
106.             }
107. 
108.             return rootFrame;
109.         }
110. 
111.         /// <summary>
112.         /// Invoked when Navigation to a certain page fails
113.         /// </summary>
114.         /// <param name="sender">The Frame which failed navigation</param>
115.         /// <param name="e">Details about the navigation failure</param>
116.         void OnNavigationFailed(object sender, NavigationFailedEventArgs e)
117.         {
118.             throw new Exception("Failed to load Page " + e.SourcePageType.FullName);
119.         }
120. 
121.         // Add any application constructor code in here.
122.         partial void Construct();
123. 
124.         // Add any OnLaunched customization here.
125.         partial void LaunchCompleted(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e);
126.     }
127. }

22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
23. 
24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }

342.         /// Initializes the MediaCapture, registers events, gets camera device information for mirroring and rotating, and starts preview.
343.         /// </summary>
344.         private async Task InitializeCameraAsync()
345.         {
346.             if (mediaCapture == null)
347.             {
348.                 // Attempt to get the back camera if one is available, but use any camera device if not.
349.                 var cameraDevice = await FindCameraDeviceByPanelAsync(Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel.Back);
350. 
351.                 if (cameraDevice == null)
352.                 {
353.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("No camera device!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
354. 
355.                     return;
356.                 }
357. 
358.                 // Create MediaCapture and its settings.
359.                 mediaCapture = new MediaCapture();
360. 
361.                 // Register for a notification when something goes wrong
362.                 mediaCapture.Failed += MediaCapture_Failed;
363. 
364.                 var settings = new MediaCaptureInitializationSettings { VideoDeviceId = cameraDevice.Id };
365. 
366.                 // Initialize MediaCapture
367.                 try
368.                 {
369.                     await mediaCapture.InitializeAsync(settings);
370.                     isInitialized = true;
371.                 }
372.                 catch (UnauthorizedAccessException)
373.                 {
374.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Denied access to the camera.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
375.                 }
376.                 catch (Exception ex)
377.                 {
378.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Exception when init MediaCapture. " + ex.Message, NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
379.                 }
380. 
381.                 // If initialization succeeded, start the preview.
382.                 if (isInitialized)
383.                 {
384.                     // Figure out where the camera is located
385.                     if (cameraDevice.EnclosureLocation == null || cameraDevice.EnclosureLocation.Panel == Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel.Unknown)
386.                     {
387.                         // No information on the location of the camera, assume it's an external camera, not integrated on the device.
388.                         externalCamera = true;
389.                     }
390.                     else
391.                     {
392.                         // Camera is fixed on the device.
393.                         externalCamera = false;
394. 
395.                         // Only mirror the preview if the camera is on the front panel.
396.                         mirroringPreview = (cameraDevice.EnclosureLocation.Panel == Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel.Front);
397.                     }
398. 
399.                     await StartPreviewAsync();
400.                 }
401.             }
402.         }
403. 
404.         /// <summary>
405.         /// Starts the preview and adjusts it for for rotation and mirroring after making a request to keep the screen on and unlocks the UI.
406.         /// </summary>
407.         private async Task StartPreviewAsync()
408.         {
409.             // Prevent the device from sleeping while the preview is running.
410.             displayRequest.RequestActive();
411. 
412.             // Set the preview source in the UI and mirror it if necessary.
413.             PreviewControl.Source = mediaCapture;
414.             PreviewControl.FlowDirection = mirroringPreview ? Microsoft.UI.Xaml.FlowDirection.RightToLeft : Microsoft.UI.Xaml.FlowDirection.LeftToRight;
415. 
416.             // Start the preview.
417.             try
418.             {
419.                 await mediaCapture.StartPreviewAsync();
420.                 isPreviewing = true;
421.             }
422.             catch (Exception ex)
423.             {
424.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Exception starting preview." + ex.Message, NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
425.             }
426. 
427.             // Initialize the preview to the current orientation.
428.             if (isPreviewing)
429.             {
430.                 await SetPreviewRotationAsync();

<a id="capture"></a>
## Camera preview: `CaptureElement` → `Image` + `SoftwareBitmapSource`

`MediaCapture` itself carries over unchanged, but WinUI 3 has **no XAML preview element**. The live preview migrates to an `<Image>` whose source is a `SoftwareBitmapSource` that you refresh from `MediaCapture` frames. This is `adaptable`, not `defer` — deferring loses the entire camera feature. Keep the [Defensive UI](SKILL.md) fallback so a device-less machine still renders a non-blank frame.

XAML — swap the element (preserve the name/`AutomationProperties` so parity checks still match):

```xml
<!-- was: <CaptureElement x:Name="PreviewControl" .../> -->
<Image x:Name="PreviewImage" Stretch="Uniform"
       AutomationProperties.Name="Camera preview"/>
```

C# — pump frames into the `Image` (`SoftwareBitmapSource` requires **Bgra8 + Premultiplied**):

```csharp
_previewSource = new SoftwareBitmapSource();
PreviewImage.Source = _previewSource;
// per frame (timer/loop, or a MediaFrameReader for higher fps):
var frame = new VideoFrame(BitmapPixelFormat.Bgra8, w, h);
await _mediaCapture.GetPreviewFrameAsync(frame);
var bmp = frame.SoftwareBitmap;
if (bmp.BitmapPixelFormat != BitmapPixelFormat.Bgra8 || bmp.BitmapAlphaMode == BitmapAlphaMode.Straight)
    bmp = SoftwareBitmap.Convert(bmp, BitmapPixelFormat.Bgra8, BitmapAlphaMode.Premultiplied);
await _previewSource.SetBitmapAsync(bmp);
```

**Face / frame analysis (`FaceDetectionEffect` → `FaceDetector`):** the UWP live `FaceDetectionEffect` (which lives in `Windows.Media.Core`, **not** `Windows.Media.FaceAnalysis` — that mismatch is the usual `CS0246`) has no CaptureElement pipeline to attach to. With the frame-pump above, run the detector per frame instead:

```csharp
_faceDetector ??= await FaceDetector.CreateAsync();          // Windows.Media.FaceAnalysis
using var gray8 = SoftwareBitmap.Convert(bmp, BitmapPixelFormat.Gray8); // DetectFacesAsync needs Gray8
var faces = await _faceDetector.DetectFacesAsync(gray8);
```

Draw the returned `FaceBox` rectangles onto a `Canvas` overlaid on the `Image`. The same "poll a frame → analyse → overlay" shape applies to other MediaCapture effects (OCR, barcode).
<shellId: 8 completed with exit code 0>
